{"parentUuid":"308d5faf-9e31-4167-8907-7ee9791184be","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"c7c0b4cd-e6c6-4488-a68b-ca4f4169315f","agentId":"afa44d52b18cbaba0","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_0117Sq4ZCyBbnwrNEhzW99eL","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"SIGIL Phase 9 W1-C — the installable, self-contained Companion PWA the owner's phone loads over\n2\tWireGuard to approve actions, panic-halt, relay commands, and view state. It holds the phone's OWN\n3\tEd25519 device key and signs LOCALLY; the desktop bridge only verifies.\n4\t\n5\tThese tests assert three things the slice must hold:\n6\t  1. all webapp files exist and are non-empty;\n7\t  2. CSP-safety — no external origins, no inline event handlers, no inline <script> bodies (the app\n8\t     is served under a strict `default-src 'self'` CSP that would break any of those);\n9\t  3. the #1 correctness item — CANONICALIZATION PARITY: the JS `canonicalJson` (canonical.js) must\n10\t     reproduce, BYTE-FOR-BYTE, the exact signed bytes the Python bridge verifies\n11\t     (sigil.bridge.envelope.envelope_message / sigil.reuse.canonical_json). This is run under Node so\n12\t     it is falsifiable, not asserted by inspection.\n13\t\n14\tRun: ~/.sigil/venv/bin/python tests/test_bridge_webapp.py\n15\t\"\"\"\n16\timport json\n17\timport re\n18\timport shutil\n19\timport subprocess\n20\tfrom pathlib import Path\n21\t\n22\tfrom sigil.bridge import build_core, envelope_message\n23\tfrom sigil.reuse import canonical_json\n24\t\n25\tfrom sigil.bridge import envelope as _E\n26\tWEBAPP = Path(_E.__file__).parent / \"webapp\"\n27\t\n28\tFILES = [\"index.html\", \"app.js\", \"style.css\", \"manifest.json\", \"service-worker.js\", \"canonical.js\"]\n29\t\n30\t\n31\tdef _read(name):\n32\t    return (WEBAPP / name).read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n33\t\n34\t\n35\t# ---- 1. every webapp file exists and is non-empty -------------------------------------------------\n36\tdef test_all_webapp_files_exist_and_nonempty():\n37\t    for name in FILES:\n38\t        p = WEBAPP / name\n39\t        assert p.is_file(), f\"missing webapp file: {name}\"\n40\t        assert p.stat().st_size > 0, f\"empty webapp file: {name}\"\n41\t\n42\t\n43\t# ---- 2a. no external origins in the JS/HTML (CSP default-src 'self') ------------------------------\n44\tdef test_no_external_origins():\n45\t    for name in (\"app.js\", \"index.html\"):\n46\t        text = _read(name)\n47\t        assert \"http://\" not in text, f\"{name} must not reference an http:// external origin\"\n48\t        assert \"https://\" not in text, f\"{name} must not reference an https:// external origin\"\n49\t\n50\t\n51\t# ---- 2b. no inline event handlers (event-delegation discipline only) -----------------------------\n52\tdef test_no_inline_event_handlers():\n53\t    for name in (\"app.js\", \"index.html\"):\n54\t        text = _read(name)\n55\t        assert \"onclick=\" not in text, f\"{name} must not use an inline onclick= handler\"\n56\t        assert \"onload=\" not in text, f\"{name} must not use an inline onload= handler\"\n57\t    # index.html: no HTML tag may carry ANY inline on*= handler attribute\n58\t    html = _read(\"index.html\")\n59\t    inline_attr = re.compile(r\"<[a-zA-Z][^>]*\\son[a-z]+\\s*=\", re.DOTALL)\n60\t    assert not inline_attr.search(html), \"index.html has an inline on*= handler attribute (CSP-unsafe)\"\n61\t\n62\t\n63\t# ---- 2c. index.html has only <script src=...> — no inline script body -----------------------------\n64\tdef test_index_scripts_are_external_only():\n65\t    html = _read(\"index.html\")\n66\t    scripts = re.findall(r\"<script\\b([^>]*)>(.*?)</script>\", html, re.DOTALL | re.IGNORECASE)\n67\t    assert scripts, \"index.html should load the app via <script src=...>\"\n68\t    for attrs, body in scripts:\n69\t        assert \"src=\" in attrs, f\"<script{attrs}> has no src= (inline script is CSP-unsafe)\"\n70\t        assert body.strip() == \"\", f\"<script{attrs}> has an inline body (CSP-unsafe): {body!r}\"\n71\t\n72\t\n73\t# ---- 2d. the JS wires behaviour via a delegated listener + holds no vendored crypto ---------------\n74\tdef test_app_uses_event_delegation_and_webcrypto_only():\n75\t    app = _read(\"app.js\")\n76\t    assert 'addEventListener(\"click\"' in app, \"app.js must wire clicks via event delegation\"\n77\t    assert \"crypto.subtle\" in app and \"Ed25519\" in app, \"app.js must use WebCrypto Ed25519\"\n78\t    # honest failure path if the browser cannot hold a device key (no silent weak-crypto fallback)\n79\t    assert \"can't hold a device key\" in app or \"cannot hold a device key\" in app, \\\n80\t        \"app.js must fail honestly when crypto.subtle lacks Ed25519\"\n81\t\n82\t\n83\t# ---- 3. CANONICALIZATION PARITY (the signed-bytes contract), verified under Node ------------------\n84\t_NODE_SCRIPT = (\n85\t    \"const {canonicalJson}=require(process.env.CANON);\"\n86\t    \"const core=JSON.parse(process.argv[1]);\"\n87\t    \"process.stdout.write(Buffer.from(canonicalJson(core),'utf8'));\"\n88\t)\n89\t\n90\t\n91\tdef _node_canonical(core: dict) -> bytes:\n92\t    \"\"\"Run the SHIPPED JS canonicalJson (canonical.js) under Node over `core`; return its UTF-8 bytes.\"\"\"\n93\t    out = subprocess.run(\n94\t        [\"node\", \"-e\", _NODE_SCRIPT, json.dumps(core)],\n95\t        env={\"CANON\": str((WEBAPP / \"canonical.js\").resolve()), \"PATH\": __import__(\"os\").environ.get(\"PATH\", \"\")},\n96\t        capture_output=True, timeout=30,\n97\t    )\n98\t    assert out.returncode == 0, f\"node canonicalJson failed: {out.stderr.decode(errors='replace')}\"\n99\t    return out.stdout\n100\t\n101\t\n102\tdef test_canonicaljson_parity_via_node():\n103\t    if not shutil.which(\"node\"):\n104\t        print(\"  SKIP  node not present — canonicalization parity NOT verified (install Node 22 to falsify)\")\n105\t        return\n106\t\n107\t    # (a) the DOCUMENTED fixed envelope vector — byte-identical to sigil.bridge.envelope.envelope_message\n108\t    core = build_core(\"DEVKEYB64\", \"read:snapshot\", {}, 1, 1700000000)\n109\t    expected = b'{\"action\":\"read:snapshot\",\"args\":{},\"device\":\"DEVKEYB64\",\"nonce\":1,\"ts\":1700000000,\"v\":1}'\n110\t    assert envelope_message(core) == expected, \"the Python parity contract byte string drifted\"\n111\t    js = _node_canonical(core)\n112\t    assert js == expected, f\"JS canonicalJson drifted from the pinned vector:\\n  py={expected!r}\\n  js={js!r}\"\n113\t    assert js == envelope_message(core), \"JS canonicalJson != Python envelope_message (signatures would be rejected)\"\n114\t\n115\t    # (b) the approval message the phone signs for /api/action — canonical_json({approver,decision,target})\n116\t    approval = {\"approver\": \"device\", \"decision\": \"approved\", \"target\": 5}\n117\t    assert _node_canonical(approval) == canonical_json(approval), \"approval-message canonicalization drifted\"\n118\t\n119\t    # (c) nested / unicode / integer edges — key sort recurses, non-ASCII stays raw, ints have no .0\n120\t    for obj in (\n121\t        {\"b\": 2, \"a\": {\"z\": [3, 2, 1], \"y\": \"x\"}, \"n\": None, \"t\": True, \"f\": False},\n122\t        {\"device\": \"abc/def+ghi==\", \"note\": \"café — naïve ✓\", \"nonce\": 42},\n123\t        {\"args\": {\"text\": \"halt now\"}, \"action\": \"relay\", \"v\": 1, \"nonce\": 7, \"ts\": 1700000123, \"device\": \"K\"},\n124\t    ):\n125\t        assert _node_canonical(obj) == canonical_json(obj), f\"canonicalization parity failed for {obj!r}\"\n126\t\n127\t\n128\tif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n129\t    fns = [v for k, v in sorted(globals().items()) if k.startswith(\"test_\")]\n130\t    passed = 0\n131\t    for fn in fns:\n132\t        try:\n133\t            fn(); print(f\"  PASS  {fn.__name__}\"); passed += 1\n134\t        except AssertionError as e:\n135\t            print(f\"  FAIL  {fn.__name__}: {e}\")\n136\t        except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001\n137\t            import traceback\n138\t            print(f\"  ERROR {fn.__name__}: {e}\")\n139\t            traceback.print_exc()\n140\t    print(f\"{passed}/{len(fns)} Phase-9 W1-C (Companion PWA) guarantees hold\")\n141\t"}]},"uuid":"3706a6f3-c182-4782-aa83-fd407a7066d8","timestamp":"2026-07-19T00:36:45.928Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"308d5faf-9e31-4167-8907-7ee9791184be","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"ee42ee4b-8404-4931-a90d-892bf0738608","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"afa44d52b18cbaba0","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-8","id":"msg_011CdAUrDMdD4KzWiYbM1Ti4","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01QUUHmygVfZLKmZzgbmQRFP","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"cd /home/kali/sigil && grep -n \"json.dump\\|json.loads\\|allow_nan\\|def append\\|def iter_records\" sigil/spine/store.py | head; echo \"=== test NaN round-trip through the spine + submit_arm_request ===\"; cat > /tmp/claude-1000/-home-kali-Pictures-PENTEST-main/7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27/scratchpad/repro_spine_nan.py <<'PY'\nimport tempfile, time, math\nfrom sigil.bridge.daemon import BridgeDaemon\nfrom sigil.gesture.session import sign_arm_request\nfrom sigil.mesh import authorize_device\nfrom sigil.reuse import generate_keypair\nfrom sigil.spine.store import SpineStore\nOWNER=generate_keypair(); DEV=generate_keypair()\ns=SpineStore(tempfile.mktemp(suffix=\".jsonl\"))\nauthorize_device(s,\"phone1\",DEV.public_key_b64,OWNER)\nd=BridgeDaemon(s,trusted_pubkey=OWNER.public_key_b64)\nreq=sign_arm_request(DEV,device_id=\"phone1\",nonce=1,ts=time.time(),ttl_seconds=float(\"nan\"))\ntry:\n    seq=d.submit_arm_request(req)\n    print(\"submit_arm_request accepted a NaN-ttl signed request, seq=\",seq)\n    # read it back from spine\n    rec=[r for r in s.iter_records() if r.payload.get(\"signal\")==\"gesture.arm_request\"][-1]\n    ttl=rec.payload.get(\"ttl_seconds\")\n    print(\"stored ttl_seconds read back from spine =\",ttl,\"isnan?\",isinstance(ttl,float) and math.isnan(ttl))\nexcept Exception as e:\n    print(\"rejected:\",type(e).__name__,e)\nPY\n~/.sigil/venv/bin/python /tmp/claude-1000/-home-kali-Pictures-PENTEST-main/7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27/scratchpad/repro_spine_nan.py","description":"Test NaN survives spine serialization + transport recording"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":618,"cache_read_input_tokens":113268,"output_tokens":2795,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":618},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":2795,"cache_read_input_tokens":113268,"cache_creation_input_tokens":618,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":618,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdAUrAyFN34mGttTdufuG","attributionAgent":"red-pen","type":"assistant","uuid":"f9cb25b3-04c1-4637-85ed-5812f7f4ab3c","timestamp":"2026-07-19T00:37:42.027Z","effort":"xhigh","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"4d9f6622-50af-4c2a-b0c4-8a64296dfcb7","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"afa44d52b18cbaba0","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-8","id":"msg_011CdAV1LoqqN1HicQDCzTb2","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_013X2EMWeTHZAArQMczs1S3A","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"cd /home/kali/sigil && cat > /tmp/claude-1000/-home-kali-Pictures-PENTEST-main/7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27/scratchpad/repro_live.py <<'PY'\nimport base64, json, tempfile, threading, time, http.client\nfrom sigil.bridge.server import build_server\nfrom sigil.bridge.envelope import build_core, sign_envelope\nfrom sigil.mesh import authorize_device\nfrom sigil.reuse import generate_keypair\nfrom sigil.spine.store import SpineStore\n\nOWNER=generate_keypair(); DEV=generate_keypair()\nsp=tempfile.mktemp(suffix=\".jsonl\")\ns=SpineStore(sp); authorize_device(s,\"phone1\",DEV.public_key_b64,OWNER)\nNOW=1_700_000_000.0\nsrv=build_server(addr=\"127.0.0.1\", port=0, spine_path=sp, trusted_pubkey=OWNER.public_key_b64, clock=lambda: NOW)\nport=srv.server_address[1]\nth=threading.Thread(target=srv.serve_forever, daemon=True); th.start()\ndef env(payload): return base64.urlsafe_b64encode(json.dumps(payload).encode()).decode().rstrip(\"=\")\n\ndef get(path, envval):\n    c=http.client.HTTPConnection(\"127.0.0.1\", port, timeout=5)\n    try:\n        c.request(\"GET\", path, headers={\"X-SIGIL-Envelope\": envval, \"Host\": f\"127.0.0.1:{port}\"})\n        r=c.getresponse(); return r.status, r.read()[:120]\n    except Exception as e:\n        return \"EXC\", f\"{type(e).__name__}: {e}\"\n    finally:\n        c.close()\n\n# 1) NEGATIVE CONTROL: a well-formed but WRONG signature -> clean 401\ngood=sign_envelope(DEV, build_core(DEV.public_key_b64,\"read:pending\",{},1,NOW))\ngood[\"sig\"]=sign_envelope(generate_keypair(), build_core(DEV.public_key_b64,\"read:pending\",{},1,NOW))[\"sig\"]  # valid-length wrong-key sig\nprint(\"wrong-key (valid-length) sig  ->\", get(\"/api/pending\", env(good)))\n\n# 2) THE BUG: authorized device pubkey + WRONG-LENGTH sig -> should be a clean 401, but crashes\nbad={**build_core(DEV.public_key_b64,\"read:pending\",{},1,NOW), \"sig\":\"AAAA\"}\nprint(\"authorized-pubkey + short sig ->\", get(\"/api/pending\", env(bad)))\n\n# 3) valid signed read -> 200 (positive control)\nok=sign_envelope(DEV, build_core(DEV.public_key_b64,\"read:pending\",{},2,NOW))\nprint(\"valid signed read             ->\", get(\"/api/pending\", env(ok)))\nsrv.shutdown()\nPY\n~/.sigil/venv/bin/python /tmp/claude-1000/-home-kali-Pictures-PENTEST-main/7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27/scratchpad/repro_live.py 2>/dev/null","description":"Live HTTP test of malformed-sig handling vs clean-refusal control"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":5573,"cache_read_input_tokens":116864,"output_tokens":2924,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":5573},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":2924,"cache_read_input_tokens":116864,"cache_creation_input_tokens":5573,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":5573,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdAV1KRkJqtbJ2r1BDLSH","attributionAgent":"red-pen","type":"assistant","uuid":"3edb5bda-e468-43f0-b065-fa4a29785fa1","timestamp":"2026-07-19T00:39:44.392Z","effort":"xhigh","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"59424550-0e46-4482-b82b-c0efa155debd","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"afa44d52b18cbaba0","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-8","id":"msg_011CdAV4YeKRK5Ukv57H8Crp","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_017QJ53Kkji5axBjADiLuzAj","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"cd /home/kali/sigil && cat > /tmp/claude-1000/-home-kali-Pictures-PENTEST-main/7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27/scratchpad/repro_toctou.py <<'PY'\nimport tempfile, threading, time\nfrom sigil.bridge.envelope import consume, build_core, sign_envelope, device_nonce_highwater, RECEIPT_SIGNAL\nfrom sigil.mesh import authorize_device\nfrom sigil.reuse import generate_keypair\nfrom sigil.spine.store import SpineStore\nOWNER=generate_keypair(); DEV=generate_keypair()\nsp=tempfile.mktemp(suffix=\".jsonl\")\ns=SpineStore(sp); authorize_device(s,\"phone1\",DEV.public_key_b64,OWNER)\nauthorized={DEV.public_key_b64}\n# ONE signed effectful envelope (nonce=5). An attacker replays it CONCURRENTLY N times.\npayload=sign_envelope(DEV, build_core(DEV.public_key_b64,\"relay\",{\"text\":\"halt\"},5,1_700_000_000.0))\nresults=[]\nbarrier=threading.Barrier(8)\ndef worker():\n    st=SpineStore(sp)   # each request opens its own store (as the server does per-request)\n    barrier.wait()      # maximize overlap of the highwater-read window\n    try:\n        consume(st, payload, authorized, effectful=True); results.append(\"ACCEPTED\")\n    except Exception as e:\n        results.append(f\"refused:{e}\")\nts=[threading.Thread(target=worker) for _ in range(8)]\n[t.start() for t in ts]; [t.join() for t in ts]\nacc=results.count(\"ACCEPTED\")\nprint(\"concurrent replays of the SAME signed nonce=5 envelope:\", results)\nprint(f\"ACCEPTED count = {acc} (a strict monotonic replay gate must accept EXACTLY 1)\")\nn_receipts=sum(1 for r in s.iter_records() if r.payload.get(\"signal\")==RECEIPT_SIGNAL and r.payload.get(\"nonce\")==5)\nprint(\"nonce=5 receipts written to spine =\", n_receipts)\nPY\n~/.sigil/venv/bin/python /tmp/claude-1000/-home-kali-Pictures-PENTEST-main/7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27/scratchpad/repro_toctou.py","description":"Test concurrent replay of the same effectful nonce (TOCTOU)"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":3036,"cache_read_input_tokens":122437,"output_tokens":2165,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":3036},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":2165,"cache_read_input_tokens":122437,"cache_creation_input_tokens":3036,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":3036,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdAV4UntoR8WdHZr5M5Yn","attributionAgent":"red-pen","type":"assistant","uuid":"8fe00ecc-9024-45bd-8574-0900149e1263","timestamp":"2026-07-19T00:40:21.055Z","effort":"xhigh","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"c13328ef-59df-4eac-b487-3a3c5dfa0d6d","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"afa44d52b18cbaba0","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-8","id":"msg_011CdAVDzKubhVuVGj4FxAJh","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Adversarial review complete. I traced every file end-to-end and built five working repros (in the scratchpad). The full suite is green (297/297 across 25 files, incl. the Node canonicalization-parity test which genuinely runs — Node v22 present). But the negative controls passing is exactly the trap: the trust-widening's tests pass because they only feed inputs the code already handles. Here is my ranked verdict.\n\n# RED-PEN: BLOCK\n\n## BLOCK-1 (HIGH) — `arm_by_device` accepts NaN/Inf, defeating BOTH the freshness gate and the MAX_DEVICE_TTL clamp (Invariant #1 d/e — the trust-widening)\n`/home/kali/sigil/sigil/gesture/session.py:148` and `:161-166`, with `Session.expired` at `:69-70`.\n\nThe freshness check is written fail-**open** for non-finite input:\n- `:148` `if abs(now - ts) > ARM_FRESHNESS:` → `abs(now - nan)` is `nan`, and `nan > 30.0` is `False`, so a NaN `ts` is **not** refused. (Contrast the envelope's `_fresh` at `server.py:146`, which uses `<= ts_window` and correctly returns `False` for NaN — the two freshness gates disagree on direction.)\n- `:162` `ttl = min(float(request.get(\"ttl_seconds\", MAX)), MAX_DEVICE_TTL)` → `min(nan, 300.0)` returns `nan`; `:165` `if ttl <= 0:` → `nan <= 0` is `False`, so it is not refused. `expires_at = now + nan = nan`, and `expired()` (`self.expires_at > 0.0 …`) is `False` forever.\n\nConfirmed against the real `SessionGate`: a signed `ttl_seconds=NaN` arm produced a session with `expires_at = nan` that still auto-injects a pointer move **100000 s later** (MAX_DEVICE_TTL is 300). A signed `ts=NaN` bypassed the 30 s freshness window entirely. `+Inf` ttl clamps correctly (control), so NaN is the specific bypass. Reachable through the transport: `submit_arm_request` accepted the NaN request and the spine round-tripped `NaN` back out (`repro_nan.py`, `repro_spine_nan.py`). This breaks the two safety bounds the doctrine calls out by name — \"bounded, never indefinite\" and \"TTL clamp — a trust-narrowing inside the widening\" — for an authorized-but-adversarial/compromised device.\nFix direction: reject non-finite `ts` and `ttl` (`math.isfinite`) before the freshness/clamp math; write the freshness gate as `not (abs(now-ts) <= ARM_FRESHNESS)`.\n\n## BLOCK-2 (MEDIUM-HIGH) — the device-armed audit record is NOT self-verifying when the TTL is clamped, and the test hides it (Invariant #1 j + Test Substance)\n`/home/kali/sigil/sigil/gesture/session.py:174` stores `\"ttl_seconds\": ttl` (the **clamped** value) while the device signature at `:138` covers the **original** requested `ttl_seconds` (it is in `_ARM_CORE`). The comment at `:168-169` and the test claim the record is \"self-verifying … tamper-evident.\"\n\nConfirmed (`repro_selfverify.py`): reconstructing `arm_request_message` from the record's own fields re-verifies for `ttl=120` (unclamped) but **fails** for `ttl=99999` (clamped→300) and for **absent ttl** (signed `None`→stored 300) — i.e. it fails for every arm above the cap, the common case. `test_gesture_arm.py:148` (`test_arm_record_is_self_verifying_against_the_device_ledger`) picks `ttl=120.0`, the one value that avoids clamping, so the green pass green-washes a property that is false for the intended input range. This is both an audit-integrity defect and a test-honesty defect.\nFix direction: store the original signed `ttl_seconds` (so the sig re-verifies) and record the effective TTL under a separate key; add a test that arms with `ttl > MAX_DEVICE_TTL` and asserts re-verification.\n\n## BLOCK-3 (MEDIUM) — read/effectful envelope auth crashes on a malformed-length signature instead of a clean refusal (Invariant #2 + #4d, honesty)\n`/home/kali/sigil/sigil/bridge/server.py:156` (`_authed`) and `:177` (`_authed_effectful`) call `verify_envelope` **unguarded**. `verify_envelope` (`envelope.py:66`) calls `verify_one`, and `_b64decode_exact` (`reuse/crypto.py:33-40`) **raises** `IntegrityError` on a wrong-length sig. The `device not in authorized` short-circuit (`envelope.py:63`) does not save you: an authorized device pubkey is **public** (it is displayed in the pairing panel and lives in the mesh ledger), so an attacker supplies a real authorized `device` + a short `sig`.\n\nConfirmed live against a running `BridgeServer` (`repro_live.py`): a wrong-key but correct-length sig → clean `401` (control); an authorized-pubkey + `\"sig\":\"AAAA\"` → `RemoteDisconnected` (unhandled exception, connection reset, no response, stderr traceback); valid sig → `200` (control). Not an auth bypass, but it contradicts the docstring's \"fail-closed … returns (False, reason)\" contract, and it is inconsistent with the POST body paths (`_device_action`/`_device_arm`) which *are* wrapped in try/except. A caller knowing only public info can force request-thread errors at will.\nFix direction: wrap `verify_envelope` in the same fail-closed try/except used for the POST bodies (catch `IntegrityError`/`Exception` → clean `401`).\n\n## BLOCK-4 (MEDIUM) — the \"strict monotonic-nonce replay gate\" is not atomic; concurrent replays of one signed effectful envelope are accepted multiple times (Invariant #2b, honesty)\n`/home/kali/sigil/sigil/bridge/envelope.py:104-106`: `consume` reads `device_nonce_highwater` then `record_receipt` with no lock, and `BridgeServer` is a `ThreadingHTTPServer` (`server.py:73`, `daemon_threads=True`).\n\nConfirmed (`repro_toctou.py`): 8 concurrent replays of the **same** signed `nonce=5` relay envelope → **3 ACCEPTED** (3 receipts on the spine); a strict monotonic gate must accept exactly 1. An attacker who captures one signed panic/relay envelope can, within the 120 s freshness window, get it executed several times. Impact is bounded (panic is idempotent; relay still passes the WARDEN gate) but the server docstring (`server.py:17-18`) advertises a \"strict monotonic-nonce replay gate,\" which is an overclaim.\nFix direction: serialize the highwater-read-then-receipt under a per-store lock (or a unique-nonce constraint), or make the append conditionally reject a duplicate `(device,nonce)` receipt.\n\n## Advisory (not independently blocking, but correct before merge for honesty)\n- The device-arm-over-transport path is **inert**: no shipped code consumes the recorded `gesture.arm_request` records via `arm_by_device` (grep confirms `arm_by_device` is called only from tests; `submit_arm_request` only records). `daemon.py:84` and `server.py:385-387` describe \"the live SessionGate … consumes this via `arm_by_device`,\" which does not exist. It fails safe today (nothing arms), which lowers BLOCK-1's *live* reachability — but the trust boundary under review is broken and would bite the instant the consumer is wired. Do not let any doc/memory claim end-to-end device remote-arm works.\n- `envelope.py:10-13` docstring says reads \"still receipt (advancing the watermark)\"; the server (`server.py:150-151`) explicitly does **not** receipt reads. Reconcile the doc.\n\n## What I attacked that HELD (with evidence)\n- **Forged/unauthorized-key arm, tampered field, revoke, stale/old-ts, single-session non-displacement, kill-switch-blocks-arm, kill-switch-neuters-mid-flight, type-queues-not-injects:** re-ran the negative control (`test_gesture_arm.py` 13/13) and re-traced each; `arm_by_device` short-circuits on `pub not in authorized`, re-verifies the sig over the signed core, and the replay check keys on the append-only `SESSION_ARMED` nonce. A device arm never displaces a live session (`:158`); `handle` consults the kill-switch fresh per intent *before* `_inject` (`:196-199`); A2 (`hid.type`/`combo`/`launch`) always queues via the fail-closed-to-A3 `KernelClassifier`. Malformed `sig`/`nonce`/`ts` into `arm_by_device` are caught (try/except at `:137-140`, `:144-147`, `:161-164`) — clean refusals, no crash (unlike the envelope path, BLOCK-3).\n- **Transport bind guard:** `BridgeServer.__init__` (`server.py:78`) and `cmd_bridge_serve` (`cli.py:446`) both assert `bind_ok` (rejects `0.0.0.0`/unspecified/public); dual-guarded.\n- **Anti-rebind:** `_rebind_ok` (`server.py:199-207`) requires exact `Host` in the WG-derived allowlist and exact `Origin` match; loopback pair added only on a loopback bind. A hostname pointing at the WG IP is refused. Reads are gated by the device signature (a rebinding page cannot forge it — the key is non-extractable and origin-isolated).\n- **Endpoint↔action binding:** every read endpoint checks `_authed(_READ_ACTION[path])`; a `read:pending` envelope cannot reach `/api/recall`. Minimal frames confirmed: `pending()`/SSE emit only `{seq,tier,kind}`.\n- **Server-side owner key:** the owner trust-root is never used to sign; `server.py:480` `.sign()` is the TLS EC key, `envelope.py:50` `sign()` is the phone/test helper. Verify-only server confirmed.\n- **Canonicalization parity:** Node parity test genuinely runs (Node v22 present, not skipped); fixed vector + unicode/nested/int cases pass.
